      '// Provides "hints" to the speech recognizer to favor specific words and phrases\n' +

      '\n' +
      '  // A phrases containing words and phrase "hints" so that\n' +
      '  // the speech recognition is more likely to recognize them. This can be used\n' +
      '  // to improve the accuracy for specific words and phrases, for example, if\n' +
      '  // specific commands are typically spoken by the user. This can also be used\n' +
      '  // to add additional words to the vocabulary of the recognizer. See\n' +
      '  // [usage limits](https://cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/quotas#content).\n' +
      '  //\n' +
      '  // List items can also include pre-built or custom classes containing groups\n' +
      '  // of words that represent common concepts that occur in natural language. For\n' +
      '  // example, rather than providing a phrase hint for every month of the\n' +
      '  // year (e.g. "i was born in january", "i was born in febuary", ...), use the\n' +
      '  // pre-built `$MONTH` class improves the likelihood of correctly transcribing\n' +
      '  // audio that includes months (e.g. "i was born in $month").\n' +
      "  // To refer to pre-built classes, use the class' symbol prepended with `$`\n" +
      '  // e.g. `$MONTH`. To refer to custom classes that were defined inline in the\n' +
      "  // request, set the class's `custom_class_id` to a string unique to all class\n" +

      '    string value = 1;\n' +
      '\n' +
      '    // Hint Boost. Overrides the boost set at the phrase set level.\n' +
      '    // Positive value will increase the probability that a specific phrase will\n' +
      '    // be recognized over other similar sounding phrases. The higher the boost,\n' +
      '    // the higher the chance of false positive recognition as well. Negative\n' +
      '    // boost will simply be ignored. Though `boost` can accept a wide range of\n' +
      '    // positive values, most use cases are best served\n' +
      '    // with values between 0 and 20. We recommend using a binary search approach\n' +
      '    // to finding the optimal value for your use case as well as adding\n' +
      '    // phrases both with and without boost to your requests.\n' +
      '    float boost = 2;\n' +
      '  }\n' +
      '\n' +
      '  // The resource name of the phrase set.\n' +
      '  string name = 1;\n' +
      '\n' +
      '  // A list of word and phrases.\n' +

      '\n' +
      '  // Hint Boost. Positive value will increase the probability that a specific\n' +
      '  // phrase will be recognized over other similar sounding phrases. The higher\n' +
      '  // the boost, the higher the chance of false positive recognition as well.\n' +
      '  // Negative boost values would correspond to anti-biasing. Anti-biasing is not\n' +
      '  // enabled, so negative boost will simply be ignored. Though `boost` can\n' +
      '  // accept a wide range of positive values, most use cases are best served with\n' +
      '  // values between 0 (exclusive) and 20. We recommend using a binary search\n' +
      '  // approach to finding the optimal value for your use case as well as adding\n' +
      '  // phrases both with and without boost to your requests.\n' +
      '  float boost = 4;\n' +
      '}\n' +
      '\n' +
      '// Speech adaptation configuration.\n' +
      'message SpeechAdaptation {\n' +
      '  message ABNFGrammar {\n' +
      '    // All declarations and rules of an ABNF grammar broken up into multiple\n' +
      '    // strings that will end up concatenated.\n' +
      '    repeated string abnf_strings = 1;\n' +
      '  }\n' +
      '\n' +
      '  // A collection of phrase sets. To specify the hints inline, leave the\n' +
      "  // phrase set's `name` blank and fill in the rest of its fields. Any\n" +
      '  // phrase set can use any custom class.\n' +
      '  repeated PhraseSet phrase_sets = 1;\n' +

      '  // A collection of phrase set resource names to use.\n' +

      '  ABNFGrammar abnf_grammar = 4;\n' +
      '}\n' +
      '\n' +
      '// Transcription normalization configuration. Use transcription normalization\n' +
      '// to automatically replace parts of the transcript with phrases of your\n' +
      '// choosing. For StreamingRecognize, this normalization only applies to stable\n' +
      '// partial transcripts (stability > 0.8) and final transcripts.\n' +

      '  // at a time. For example, the second entry in ["cat" => "dog", "mountain cat"\n' +

      '  // first entry before it. At most 100 entries.\n' +

      '    // Provides information to the recognizer that specifies how to process the\n' +
      '    // request. The first `StreamingRecognizeRequest` message must contain a\n' +
      '    // `streaming_config`  message.\n' +
      '    StreamingRecognitionConfig streaming_config = 1;\n' +
      '\n' +
      '    // The audio data to be recognized. Sequential chunks of audio data are sent\n' +
      '    // in sequential `StreamingRecognizeRequest` messages. The first\n' +
      '    // `StreamingRecognizeRequest` message must not contain `audio_content` data\n' +
      '    // and all subsequent `StreamingRecognizeRequest` messages must contain\n' +
      '    // `audio_content` data. The audio bytes must be encoded as specified in\n' +
      '    // `RecognitionConfig`. Note: as with all bytes fields, proto buffers use a\n' +
      '    // pure binary representation (not base64). See\n' +
      '    // [content limits](https://cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/quotas#content).\n' +
      '    bytes audio_content = 2;\n' +
      '  }\n' +
      '}\n' +

      '// request.\n' +
      'message StreamingRecognitionConfig {\n' +
      '  // Events that a timeout can be set on for voice activity.\n' +
      '  message VoiceActivityTimeout {\n' +
      '    // Duration to timeout the stream if no speech begins.\n' +
      '    google.protobuf.Duration speech_start_timeout = 1;\n' +
      '\n' +
      '    // Duration to timeout the stream after speech ends.\n' +
      '    google.protobuf.Duration speech_end_timeout = 2;\n' +
      '  }\n' +
      '\n' +

      '  // process the request.\n' +

      '\n' +
      '  // If `false` or omitted, the recognizer will perform continuous\n' +
      '  // recognition (continuing to wait for and process audio even if the user\n' +
      '  // pauses speaking) until the client closes the input stream (gRPC API) or\n' +
      '  // until the maximum time limit has been reached. May return multiple\n' +
      '  // `StreamingRecognitionResult`s with the `is_final` flag set to `true`.\n' +
      '  //\n' +
      '  // If `true`, the recognizer will detect a single spoken utterance. When it\n' +
      '  // detects that the user has paused or stopped speaking, it will return an\n' +
      '  // `END_OF_SINGLE_UTTERANCE` event and cease recognition. It will return no\n' +
      '  // more than one `StreamingRecognitionResult` with the `is_final` flag set to\n' +
      '  // `true`.\n' +
      '  //\n' +
      '  // The `single_utterance` field can only be used with specified models,\n' +
      '  // otherwise an error is thrown. The `model` field in\n' +
      '  // [RecognitionConfig][google.cloud.speech.v1p1beta1.RecognitionConfig] must\n' +

      '  //\n' +
      '  // * `command_and_search`\n' +
      '  // * `phone_call` AND additional field `useEnhanced`=`true`\n' +
      '  // * The `model` field is left undefined. In this case the API auto-selects\n' +
      '  //   a model based on any other parameters that you set in\n' +
      '  //   `RecognitionConfig`.\n' +

      '// Provides "hints" to the speech recognizer to favor specific words and phrases\n' +
      '// in the results.\n' +
      'message PhraseSet {\n' +
      '  option (google.api.resource) = {\n' +
      '    type: "speech.googleapis.com/PhraseSet"\n' +
      '    pattern: "projects/{project}/locations/{location}/phraseSets/{phrase_set}"\n' +
      '  };\n' +
      '\n' +
      '  // A phrases containing words and phrase "hints" so that\n' +
      '  // the speech recognition is more likely to recognize them. This can be used\n' +
      '  // to improve the accuracy for specific words and phrases, for example, if\n' +
      '  // specific commands are typically spoken by the user. This can also be used\n' +
      '  // to add additional words to the vocabulary of the recognizer. See\n' +
      '  // [usage limits](https://cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/quotas#content).\n' +
      '  //\n' +
      '  // List items can also include pre-built or custom classes containing groups\n' +
      '  // of words that represent common concepts that occur in natural language. For\n' +
      '  // example, rather than providing a phrase hint for every month of the\n' +
      '  // year (e.g. "i was born in january", "i was born in febuary", ...), use the\n' +
      '  // pre-built `$MONTH` class improves the likelihood of correctly transcribing\n' +
      '  // audio that includes months (e.g. "i was born in $month").\n' +
      "  // To refer to pre-built classes, use the class' symbol prepended with `$`\n" +
      '  // e.g. `$MONTH`. To refer to custom classes that were defined inline in the\n' +
      "  // request, set the class's `custom_class_id` to a string unique to all class\n" +
      "  // resources and inline classes. Then use the class' id wrapped in $`{...}`\n" +
      '  // e.g. "${my-months}". To refer to custom classes resources, use the class\'\n' +

      '  //\n' +
      '  // Speech-to-Text supports three locations: `global`, `us` (US North America),\n' +
      '  // and `eu` (Europe). If you are calling the `speech.googleapis.com`\n' +
      '  // endpoint, use the `global` location. To specify a region, use a\n' +
      '  // [regional endpoint](https://cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/endpoints)\n' +

      '  message Phrase {\n' +
      '    // The phrase itself.\n' +
      '    string value = 1;\n' +

      '    // Hint Boost. Overrides the boost set at the phrase set level.\n' +

      '    // be recognized over other similar sounding phrases. The higher the boost,\n' +
      '    // the higher the chance of false positive recognition as well. Negative\n' +

      '    // positive values, most use cases are best served\n' +

      '    // to finding the optimal value for your use case as well as adding\n' +
      '    // phrases both with and without boost to your requests.\n' +

      '  // Negative boost values would correspond to anti-biasing. Anti-biasing is not\n' +
